# Max Verstappen

"Verstappen" redirects here. For other people with this surname, see [Verstappen (surname)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verstappen_\(surname\)). For his father, see [Jos Verstappen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos_Verstappen).

**Max Emilian Verstappen** (Dutch pronunciation: [\[ˈmɑks fɛrˈstɑpə(n)\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Dutch); born 30 September 1997) is a Dutch and Belgian [racing driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_driver) who competes [under the Dutch flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_the_Netherlands) in [Formula One](10854.md) for [Red Bull Racing](1173193.md). Verstappen has won four [Formula One World Drivers' Championship titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_World_Drivers'_Championship_titles), which he won consecutively from [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_One_World_Championship) to [2024](72504121.md) with Red Bull, and has won 71 [Grands Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_Grands_Prix) across 12 seasons.

Born in [Hasselt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasselt) and raised in [Maaseik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maaseik), Verstappen is the son of Dutch former Formula One driver [Jos Verstappen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos_Verstappen) and Belgian former [kart racer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racer) [Sophie Kumpen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophie_Kumpen). After a successful karting career—culminating in his [record-breaking 2013 season](#2013:_Record-breaking_season)—Verstappen graduated to [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae). Progressing directly to [FIA European Formula 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_European_Championship), Verstappen broke several records on his way to third in the championship in [his rookie season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIA_Formula_3_European_Championship) with [Van Amersfoort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Amersfoort).[^cite_note-3] Aged 17, Verstappen signed for [Toro Rosso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toro_Rosso) in [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Formula_One_World_Championship) as part of the [Red Bull Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Junior_Team), becoming the [youngest driver in Formula One history](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_start_a_race) at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Grand_Prix). Following several points finishes in his debut season, Verstappen retained his seat for [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Formula_One_World_Championship) before being promoted to parent team [Red Bull](1173193.md) to partner [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo) after four rounds. On debut for Red Bull, aged 18, Verstappen won the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Spanish_Grand_Prix), becoming the [youngest-ever driver to win a Formula One Grand Prix](13599687.md#Youngest_winners). Verstappen achieved multiple race wins in his [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Formula_One_World_Championship) and [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Formula_One_World_Championship) campaigns, before finishing third in both the [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_One_World_Championship) and [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Formula_One_World_Championship) World Drivers' Championships [under Honda power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One).

Verstappen won his maiden title in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_One_World_Championship) after overtaking [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) on the final lap of the [last race of the season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix), becoming the [first World Drivers' Champion from the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_the_Netherlands). Verstappen won the next two championships in [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Formula_One_World_Championship) and [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship), overturning the largest points deficit in Formula One history in the former and breaking numerous records across both seasons.[^cite_note-4] He secured his fourth consecutive title in [2024](72504121.md) after winning nine Grands Prix, including a widely acclaimed wet-weather performance in [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix), to become the first driver to win the championship driving for a third-placed constructor in [41 years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Formula_One_World_Championship). He finished runner-up by two points in a three-way title battle with [Lando Norris](48978813.md) and [Oscar Piastri](59449712.md) in [2025](72768531.md).

As of the [2026 Bahrain Grand Prix](80201648.md), Verstappen has achieved 71 race wins, 49 pole positions, 37 fastest laps, and 134 podiums in Formula One. In addition to being the youngest Grand Prix winner, he holds several [Formula One records](13599687.md), including the [most wins in a season](13599687.md#Most_wins_in_a_season) (19), the [most podium finishes in a season](13599687.md#Most_podium_finishes_in_a_season) (21), the [most consecutive wins](13599687.md#Most_consecutive_wins) (10), and the [most consecutive pole positions](13599687.md#Most_consecutive_pole_positions) (8, shared with [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna)). Verstappen is contracted to remain at Red Bull until at least the end of the 2030 season. Outside of Formula One, Verstappen runs the [Verstappen Racing](#Verstappen_Racing) team, has competed in [sports car racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_car_racing) events, and has also competed professionally in [sim racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sim_racing) since 2015, winning several marquee *[iRacing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRacing)* events. Verstappen was listed in the 2024 issue of *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* as one of the [100 most influential people globally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100), and was appointed an [Officer of the Order of Orange-Nassau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Officer_of_the_Order_of_Orange-Nassau) in 2022.

## Early life

Max Emilian Verstappen was born on 30 September 1997 in [Hasselt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasselt), Belgium, to [Jos Verstappen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos_Verstappen) and [Sophie Kumpen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophie_Kumpen).[^cite_note-5] Verstappen's parents separated when he was young, after which he lived with his father. His younger sister, Victoria, lived with their mother.[^cite_note-6] Verstappen has three younger half-siblings from his father: a sister from Jos's second marriage and a brother and sister from Jos's current marriage.[^cite_note-\:2-7]

His family has a long association with motor sports: his father is a Dutch former Formula One driver, his Belgian mother competed in [karting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racing),[^cite_note-racing_family2-8][^cite_note-nationality2-9] and his first cousin once removed, [Anthony Kumpen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Kumpen), competed in endurance racing and is a two-time [NASCAR Whelen Euro Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASCAR_Whelen_Euro_Series) champion currently serving as the team manager for [PK Carsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PK_Carsport) in Euro Series.[^cite_note-racing_family2-8] Verstappen initially attended a secondary school in [Maaseik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maaseik), before moving into [private tutoring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_tutor); he admitted that he used to [leave class early](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truancy) to attend races across Europe with his father.[^cite_note-10]

## Junior racing career

### Karting (2005–2013)

#### 2005–2009: Dominance in national championships

Verstappen started racing in karts at the age of four and competing in championships at the age of seven. In 2005 he competed in the Mini Junior championship of his home province of [Limburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limburg_\(Belgium\)), Belgium,[^cite_note-11] and won the [VAS](10878.md) Championship in the same category. In 2006, he repeated his success. In 2007, Verstappen graduated to the [Rotax Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotax_Max) Mini Max class and won the Rotax Max Challenge Belgium[^cite_note-12] and the Dutch Championship[^cite_note-13] in that category. In 2008, racing in a [CRG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CRG_\(kart_manufacturer\)) kart entered by his father, Verstappen won the Belgian Cadet Championship,[^cite_note-14] the Rotax Max Challenge Belgium,[^cite_note-15] and the Benelux Karting Series in Mini Max class.[^cite_note-16] In 2009, Verstappen joined Pex Racing, a CRG customer team. That year, he defended his titles in the Mini Max championships[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] and won the Belgian KF5 Championship.[^cite_note-19]

#### 2010–2012: Entering the international karting scene

Verstappen was signed by CRG to race in their factory team and stepped up to international karting in 2010. At the [KF3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF3) [World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Karting_World_Championship), Verstappen finished second to the more experienced [Alexander Albon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Albon) (who eventually became his Formula One teammate at Red Bull Racing)[^cite_note-20] but beat him at the [WSK Euro Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_WSK_Euro_Series)[^cite_note-21] and also won the WSK World Series, beating [Robert Vișoiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Vi%C8%99oiu).[^cite_note-22] He finished fifth in the [European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIK-FIA_Karting_European_Championship) and won the WSK Nations Cup[^cite_note-23] as well as the final of the Bridgestone Cup where during a 28 laps' race in the rain he beat [Dennis Olsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Olsen_\(racing_driver\)) by over 11 seconds.[^cite_note-24] In 2011, Verstappen won the [WSK Euro Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_WSK_Euro_Series) ahead of [Esteban Ocon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esteban_Ocon) in a [Parilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_American_Motor_Engineering)-powered CRG kart.[^cite_note-25] However, it was not a successful year for him, as he finished 14th in the European Championship and DNF in the [World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Karting_World_Championship) after an electrical failure while he and [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc) were catching the drivers in front for the win.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27]

In the following year, Verstappen joined the Intrepid Driver Program to race in the [KF2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF2) and [KZ2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KZ2) classes. He won the [WSK Master Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Super_Master_Series) in the KF2 class, beating the CRG driver Felice Tiene.[^cite_note-28] Verstappen won the South Garda Winter Cup in the KF2 class, beating [Dennis Olsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Olsen_\(racing_driver\)) and [Antonio Fuoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Fuoco).[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30] He finished sixth in the [WSK Euro Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Euro_Series).[^cite_note-31] He was the youngest driver taking part in the World Championship in [KF1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF1) class and finished eighth.[^cite_note-32] In July 2012, it was announced that Verstappen would leave Intrepid. After a short stint with CRG-built [Zanardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Zanardi) karts, Verstappen returned to the factory CRG team.[^cite_note-33] He finished second at the World Cup in the KF2 class[^cite_note-34] and competed at the SKUSA SuperNationals in the KZ2 class in a CRG, finishing 21st.

#### 2013: Record-breaking season

In 2013, Verstappen completed a record-breaking season in karting, having progressed to the senior [KF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF2) and [KZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KZ_\(karting\)) categories. Aged 15, he won three [CIK-FIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIK-FIA) championships in a single season: two [European Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_European_Championship) and a [World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_World_Championship); an unprecedented feat in the history of the discipline.[^cite_note-35] He became the first driver since his father [Jos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos_Verstappen) to win two European Championships in the same season, and the first to do so in both primary classes. By winning the 2013 [KZ World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_World_Championship#Gearbox_class_\(1983%E2%80%932000,_2013%E2%80%93present\)) at [Varennes-sur-Allier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varennes-sur-Allier), beating [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc) to the title, Verstappen became the youngest-ever driver to win the gearbox World Championship.[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37]

Further adding to his accolades in 2013, Verstappen also finished third at the [KF World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_World_Championship#FIA_Karting_World_champions_\(direct_drive_&_primary_category\)) after being disqualified for a move on [Nicklas Nielsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicklas_Nielsen) in the final race, having won the opening round and pre-final.[^cite_note-38] This was the only championship he failed to win in 2013 whilst completing all rounds. Verstappen won the South Garda Winter Cup in [KF2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF2),[^cite_note-39] as well as the [WSK Euro Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Euro_Series) and the [WSK Super Master Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Super_Master_Series) in [KZ1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KZ1_\(karting\)) and [KZ2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KZ2), respectively.[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-41]

### Lower formulae (2014)





Verstappen competed in the [FIA Formula 3 European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_European_Championship) in [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIA_Formula_3_European_Championship), after joining the [Red Bull Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Junior_Team).

Verstappen first experienced [formula racing](1618495.md) at [Pembrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pembrey_Circuit) in August 2013 with [Manor MP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MP_Motorsport); he drove 160 laps across two days in a [Barazi-Epsilon FR2.0–10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barazi-Epsilon#Barazi-Epsilon_FR2.0-10).[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-43] He tested for several other [Formula Renault 2.0](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Renault_2.0) teams that year.[^cite_note-44] In December 2013, Verstappen tested a [Dallara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallara) F311 [Formula Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Three) car run by [Motopark Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motopark_Academy).[^cite_note-45] An additional Formula Renault test followed in December at [Circuito de Jerez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuito_de_Jerez). Driving for [Josef Kaufmann Racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josef_Kaufmann_Racing), Verstappen went faster than Formula Renault regulars like [Steijn Schothorst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steijn_Schothorst) and [Matt Parry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Parry).[^cite_note-46] At the [Circuit Ricardo Tormo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Ricardo_Tormo) near [Valencia](63861.md), Verstappen set a faster lap time than the more experienced driver [Eddie Cheever III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddie_Cheever_III).[^cite_note-47]

#### 2014: Florida Winter Series and Formula Three

On 16 January 2014, it was announced Verstappen would make his [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae) debut in the non-championship [Florida Winter Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florida_Winter_Series).[^cite_note-48] On 5 February, at the second race weekend, Verstappen won the race at [Palm Beach International Raceway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Beach_International_Raceway) after he started from pole.[^cite_note-49] A few weeks later, Verstappen won his second race of the series at [Homestead–Miami Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homestead%E2%80%93Miami_Speedway) after beating [Nicholas Latifi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Latifi) by 0.004 seconds.[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51]

Following his single-seater debut in the inaugural Florida Winter Series, Verstappen drove in the [2014 FIA Formula 3 European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIA_Formula_3_European_Championship) for [Van Amersfoort Racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Amersfoort_Racing).[^cite_note-52] Aged sixteen, Verstappen achieved ten victories in total—including a record six consecutive wins at [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa-Francorchamps) and the [Norisring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norisring)—along with eight retirements and one missed start, becoming the youngest race winner and polesitter in [Formula Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Three) history at the [Hockenheimring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockenheimring); he placed third in the overall standings, with the most wins.[^cite_note-53][^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56]

## Formula One career

During the first practice session at the [2014 Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Japanese_Grand_Prix), Verstappen replaced [Jean-Éric Vergne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-%C3%89ric_Vergne) as part of his preparation for a full-time seat at [Toro Rosso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Toro_Rosso) in the [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Formula_One_World_Championship) season.[^cite_note-57] Aged 17 years and three days, Verstappen was the youngest person in history to participate in a Formula One race weekend.[^cite_note-58] In August 2014, Verstappen joined the [Red Bull Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Junior_Team) after testing a Formula Renault 3.5 car. He also considered an offer from [Mercedes](25098794.md) to join their [driver development programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_Junior_Team).[^cite_note-59]

### Toro Rosso (2015–2016)



Verstappen (pictured at the [2015 Malaysian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Malaysian_Grand_Prix)) became the youngest driver in Formula One history at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Grand_Prix), aged 17.

Verstappen then became the [youngest driver to start a World Championship race](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_start_a_race) through joining Toro Rosso's race drivers' line-up with [Carlos Sainz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Sainz_Jr.) as his teammate, in his Grand Prix debut as a full-time driver at the [2015 Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Australian_Grand_Prix) at the age of 17 years, 166 days—breaking [Jaime Alguersuari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaime_Alguersuari)'s existing record by almost two years.[^cite_note-60] In this first race, Verstappen ran in points-scoring positions until he was forced to retire due to an engine failure.[^cite_note-61] However, at the subsequent race in [Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Malaysian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified sixth and finished the race in seventh place, scoring his first Formula One points aged 17 years, 180 days, breaking the record of the then-[youngest driver to score World Championship points](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_score_points).[^cite_note-62]

At the [2015 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Monaco_Grand_Prix), Verstappen was involved in a high-speed collision with [Romain Grosjean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romain_Grosjean), after clipping the back of Grosjean's [Lotus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotus_F1) on the approach to the tight first corner, Sainte Devote, and crashed into the barriers at high speed.[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-64] Verstappen was given a five-place grid penalty for causing the accident, and was branded "dangerous" by [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Grand_Prix_Engineering) driver [Felipe Massa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_Massa).[^cite_note-65] Verstappen continued to regularly finish in the points as well as achieving his best finish of the 2015 season in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) by finishing fourth,[^cite_note-66] and equalled this result at the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_United_States_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-67] At the end of the season, Verstappen received three awards at the [FIA Prize Giving Ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Prize_Giving_Ceremony), for "Rookie of the Year", "Personality of the Year" and "Action of the Year", for his overtake on [Felipe Nasr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_Nasr) on the outside of the Blanchimont corner at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Belgian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69]



Verstappen at the [2016 Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Bahrain_Grand_Prix)

Verstappen began the 2016 season at Toro Rosso, again alongside Sainz.[^cite_note-70] Verstappen qualified fifth for the opening race of the season in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Australian_Grand_Prix), but during the race made several radio calls to his team due to frustration at being behind Sainz on track before Verstappen's Toro Rosso came into contact with his teammate's car whilst attempting to pass him with three laps to go, and he eventually finished tenth.[^cite_note-71] Verstappen enjoyed a more successful weekend at the following race in [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), finishing sixth to score Toro Rosso's first-ever points at the Sakhir circuit.[^cite_note-72]

### Red Bull (2016–present)

#### 2016: Youngest Grand Prix winner



Verstappen celebrating victory at the [2016 Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Spanish_Grand_Prix), in his first race for the Red Bull Racing team

On 5 May 2016, following the [Russian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Russian_Grand_Prix), Red Bull Racing announced that Verstappen would be replacing Daniil Kvyat from the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Spanish_Grand_Prix) onwards, with Kvyat returning to Toro Rosso.[^cite_note-73] According to Red Bull Team Principal [Christian Horner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Horner), "Max has proven to be an outstanding young talent. His performance at Toro Rosso has been impressive so far and we are pleased to give him the opportunity to drive for Red Bull Racing."[^cite_note-RBR_Swap-74] After qualifying fourth for the Spanish Grand Prix,[^cite_note-Verstappen_4th_in_Qualifying-75] Verstappen rose to second behind teammate [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo) on the opening lap after Mercedes teammates [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) and [Nico Rosberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_Rosberg) crashed out of the race.[^cite_note-76] Verstappen took the race lead as he was placed on a two-stop rather than the same three-stop strategy as Ricciardo, and he held off [Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Ferrari)'s [Kimi Räikkönen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_R%C3%A4ikk%C3%B6nen) in the later stages of the race to take his first Formula One victory.[^cite_note-77][^cite_note-78] By doing so he displaced [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel) as the youngest driver ever to win a Formula One Grand Prix at the age of 18 years and 228 days.[^cite_note-\:0-79] In his first eight races with Red Bull, he achieved six top-five finishes, including four podiums.[^cite_note-Standings-80]



Verstappen on the podium with [Daniel Ricciardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Ricciardo) and [Nico Rosberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_Rosberg) at the [2016 Malaysian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Malaysian_Grand_Prix)

During the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Belgian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen collided with Räikkönen at the first corner, pushed Vettel, Räikkönen and Pérez wide at *Les Combes*, and aggressively blocked Räikkönen on the Kemmel straight.[^cite_note-81] Verstappen was criticised for his driving, with Räikkönen saying that he was "going to cause a huge accident sooner or later". Christian Horner noted that the driving was "on the edge", and that Verstappen will "look at it and learn for future races".[^cite_note-82] In September, Formula One director [Charlie Whiting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Whiting) called in Verstappen for a discussion, and later gave him a 'gentle warning' due to his aggressive driving.[^cite_note-83] In October, drivers' concerns about Verstappen's defensive tactics led the FIA to disallow moving under braking.[^cite_note-84]

At the [2016 Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Brazilian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified fourth.[^cite_note-85] In a rain affected race, he almost hit the barrier after he slid on the main straight due to a loss of traction, causing [oversteer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oversteer).[^cite_note-86] After an additional tyre change from intermediates back to rain tyres, he ran in 16th place with just 15 laps remaining. Verstappen then made several overtakes in quick succession during the closing laps to eventually finish on the podium in third place.[^cite_note-87] He received considerable praise for his performance: rival team Mercedes' team principal, [Toto Wolff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toto_Wolff), labelled it "The Verstappen Show", and described Verstappen's drive as "physics..being redefined".[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] However, Verstappen came under criticism from four-time world champion Sebastian Vettel, who stated that Verstappen had pushed him off the track at the Junção corner late in the race. The race stewards did not share Vettel's view and decided that no reprimand was warranted.[^cite_note-91]

#### 2017–2018: Reliability issues and rise to prominence

Verstappen overtaking Lewis Hamilton for the lead of the 2017 Malaysian Grand Prix

Verstappen took his second career victory at the [Malaysian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Malaysian_Grand_Prix) in [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Formula_One_World_Championship).

&#x20;During the first 14 races of the 2017 season, Verstappen suffered seven retirements, four due to mechanical issues,[^cite_note-92] and three due to first lap collisions in [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Spanish_Grand_Prix), [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Austrian_Grand_Prix) and [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Singapore_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-93] Of the races he finished, however, he claimed a third place in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Chinese_Grand_Prix), and in another five races he finished fourth or fifth.[^cite_note-Standings-80] From the [Malaysian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Malaysian_Grand_Prix) onward, Verstappen enjoyed a surge of success. He won his second Formula One race at the 2017 Malaysian Grand Prix, a day after his 20th birthday, passing then three-time champion Lewis Hamilton for the lead in the early stages of the race.[^cite_note-94] He finished second in the following [race in Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Japanese_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-95] He then finished third at the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_United_States_Grand_Prix) Grand Prix, but was classified fourth after his final lap overtake on Kimi Räikkönen was deemed illegal.[^cite_note-96] He won his third Formula One race at the [Mexican Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Mexican_Grand_Prix), after passing [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel) on the opening lap.[^cite_note-97]



Verstappen at the [2018 Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Spanish_Grand_Prix)

In the first six races of the [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Formula_One_World_Championship) season, Verstappen had been involved in at least one incident in each race. In [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Australian_Grand_Prix), he qualified fourth but fell behind [Kevin Magnussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Magnussen) at the start.[^cite_note-98] In his attempts to retake the position he ran wide multiple times and damaged his car, with a spin causing him to fall further down the order. He recovered to eventually finish the race in sixth place.[^cite_note-99] At the next race in [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), he crashed during qualifying and started in 15th place.[^cite_note-100] He had a productive first lap after which he found himself in the points while challenging Lewis Hamilton. He attempted an overtake on the reigning World Champion at the start of lap two, but collided with the Mercedes driver and suffered a puncture that ultimately led to suspension damage, forcing him out of the race.[^cite_note-101]

At the next race in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Chinese_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified fifth and had moved up to third at the end of the first lap.[^cite_note-102] Both Red Bull drivers pitted for fresh tyres during a safety car which left them with a tyre advantage over the front-runners ahead. In an overtake attempt on [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel) for third place, Verstappen collided with the championship leader, causing him to fall to eighth and receive a 10-second penalty.[^cite_note-103] He recovered to fourth place, with his penalty causing him to be classified fifth. Teammate Ricciardo went on to win the race.[^cite_note-104] In [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix), Verstappen was embroiled in a race-long battle with Ricciardo for fourth place. After numerous position changes between the two teammates during the race, Ricciardo ran into the back of Verstappen during an overtake attempt from which the Dutchman aggressively defended, causing the retirement of both cars.[^cite_note-105] Both drivers were blamed by the team and reprimanded by the stewards.[^cite_note-106] Verstappen bounced back in [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Spanish_Grand_Prix) with his first podium of the season by finishing third behind the Mercedes drivers, holding off [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel). However, the race was also not without incident as he had run into the back of [Lance Stroll](42274711.md) during the virtual safety car period, causing minor front wing damage.[^cite_note-107]

In [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Monaco_Grand_Prix), Verstappen made another error and crashed near the end of the third free practice session in an incident which closely resembled a crash he had at the same spot [two years earlier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Monaco_Grand_Prix#Qualifying).[^cite_note-108] His team could not repair his car in time for qualifying and Verstappen had to start the race from the back of the grid.[^cite_note-\:1-109] Verstappen did not compete and did not set a lap time in qualifying, therefore teammate Ricciardo pressed home Red Bull's advantage at the track by taking [pole position](630713.md) and the race win. Verstappen managed to salvage two points by finishing ninth place, overtaking six cars on track.[^cite_note-110] Team principal Christian Horner commented on Verstappen's start of the season, saying he "needed to stop making these mistakes" and that he could "learn from his teammate", while [Helmut Marko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmut_Marko), head of driver development at Red Bull, said that Verstappen was "too impatient".[^cite_note-\:1-109] Verstappen now lay in sixth place in the championship with 35 points, only three points ahead of [Fernando Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Alonso) in the McLaren, and 37 points behind his teammate in third, who had taken two wins in the first six races.[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112]



Verstappen celebrating on the podium with [Kimi Räikkönen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_R%C3%A4ikk%C3%B6nen) and [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel) after winning the [2018 Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Austrian_Grand_Prix)

In [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Canadian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen topped all three practice sessions and qualified third, two-tenths off Sebastian Vettel in pole position.[^cite_note-113] He eventually finished third and set the fastest lap of the race.[^cite_note-114] The following race in [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_French_Grand_Prix) brought him second place.[^cite_note-115] In [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Austrian_Grand_Prix)—Red Bull's home track, he started fourth on the grid, passed Kimi Räikkönen before taking advantage of retirement from [Valtteri Bottas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valtteri_Bottas) and a botched pit-stop strategy by Lewis Hamilton, who later had to retire from fourth place, to claim the fourth race victory of his career.[^cite_note-116] In [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_British_Grand_Prix), Verstappen was plagued by issues, finishing the first practice session early due to a gearbox problem and crashing in the second practice session before retiring from the race due to a brake problem.[^cite_note-117] He would then finish fourth in [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_German_Grand_Prix) after strategy errors let a recovering Hamilton get past him as he went on to win the race.[^cite_note-118] Verstappen ended the first half of the season with a retirement in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) and was narrowly behind his teammate in the championship due to his own recent resurgence and Ricciardo's unreliability.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120]

Verstappen enjoyed a very strong second half of the season, achieving podium finishes in [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Belgian_Grand_Prix), [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Singapore_Grand_Prix), [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Japanese_Grand_Prix) and the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_United_States_Grand_Prix), the latter of which he achieved second place having started from 18th on the grid due to a suspension failure in qualifying.[^cite_note-121] Following the qualifying session at the [Mexican Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Mexican_Grand_Prix), Verstappen revealed that a mechanical problem with his Red Bull under braking cost him any chance of becoming the youngest-ever Formula One pole-sitter. As a result, Ricciardo beat Verstappen to pole position by just 0.026 seconds.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] Verstappen had a better start than Ricciardo and took the lead of the race into the first corner, overtaking the fast-starting Mercedes of Lewis Hamilton. Verstappen earned his fifth career win in Mexico.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] He was poised to win the [2018 Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brazilian_Grand_Prix), having overtaken Räikkönen, Vettel, Bottas, and Hamilton. However, he collided with [Force India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Force_India) driver [Esteban Ocon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esteban_Ocon) who was trying to unlap himself on faster tyres.[^cite_note-126] Ocon received a 10-second stop-and-go penalty for the incident. After the collision with Ocon, Verstappen finished in second place behind Hamilton. During an argument with Ocon after the race, Verstappen pushed the Force India driver, for which he was given two days of "public service" as a penalty by the FIA.[^cite_note-127] He then finished his season with another podium as he finished third in Abu Dhabi.[^cite_note-128] Verstappen ended the season in fourth place in the championship with 249 points, claiming two wins, eleven podium finishes, and two fastest laps.[^cite_note-129]

#### 2019–2020: Dominating teammates



Verstappen at the [2019 Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), where he started on pole position for the first time in his career

In [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_One_World_Championship), Red Bull switched from Renault to [Honda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One) power units.[^cite_note-130] After Ricciardo moved to the [Renault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_in_Formula_One) team for 2019, Verstappen was joined at Red Bull by [Pierre Gasly](40843850.md).[^cite_note-131] Verstappen qualified in fourth and finished third in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Grand_Prix), the first podium finish for a [Honda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_in_Formula_One)-powered driver since the [2008 British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_British_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-132] Verstappen was on course for a second third-place finish in [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Bahrain_Grand_Prix) before a late safety car prevented him from overtaking Charles Leclerc's ailing Ferrari, keeping him in fourth place.[^cite_note-133] Two more fourth-place finishes followed in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Chinese_Grand_Prix) and [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix), and a podium in [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Spanish_Grand_Prix) in third place.[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] In [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Monaco_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified in third place. He was released into the path of Valtteri Bottas during the drivers' pit stops, gaining second place but receiving a 5-second penalty as a result. Verstappen crossed the line in second place but was demoted to fourth by the penalty.[^cite_note-137]

In [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Canadian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen's final lap in the second qualifying session was hampered by a red flag brought out by [Kevin Magnussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Magnussen)'s crash. This caused Verstappen to qualify 11th and start the race in ninth place.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139] He later recovered to finish fifth.[^cite_note-140] In [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_French_Grand_Prix) he started and finished in fourth place.[^cite_note-141] In [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Austrian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen started third but suffered a poor start, dropping down to eighth. After a charge towards the front, he made his way up to second before controversially passing Leclerc for the lead of the race with three laps to go.[^cite_note-142] This marked the first Honda-powered race victory since the [2006 Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Hungarian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] In [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_British_Grand_Prix), Verstappen, running in third place,[^cite_note-145] was hit from behind during an overtake attempt by Sebastian Vettel and spun into the gravel. Verstappen was able to continue and crossed the line in fifth place.[^cite_note-146]

The wet and chaotic [German Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_German_Grand_Prix) began similarly to the race in Austria for Verstappen, as a poor start caused him to fall behind. However, he would inherit the lead midway through the race after a crash by race leader Hamilton.[^cite_note-147] Verstappen would go on to extend his lead after the track began to dry, claiming his second victory of the season.[^cite_note-148] In [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), he claimed his maiden [pole position](24385119.md)—becoming the [first Dutch driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_the_Netherlands) to do so—and led most of the race before being passed in the closing laps by Hamilton, who had made another stop for fresh tyres in a gamble to catch the leader.[^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150][^cite_note-151]



Verstappen on the podium after winning the [2019 Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Brazilian_Grand_Prix)

Before the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Belgian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen received a new teammate in Alexander Albon after Pierre Gasly was demoted back to Toro Rosso.[^cite_note-152] In the race, Verstappen had a poor start and collided with Kimi Räikkönen at the first corner, resulting in suspension damage and causing Verstappen's first retirement of the season.[^cite_note-153] In [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Italian_Grand_Prix), he did not set a time during qualifying after his car lost power in Q1, but he was already required to start from the back of the grid due to an engine component penalty.[^cite_note-154] After damaging his front wing on the first lap, he recovered to finish the race in eighth place.[^cite_note-155] Third and fourth-place finishes followed in [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Singapore_Grand_Prix) and [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Russian_Grand_Prix) respectively.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] After suffering damage in a first-lap collision with Charles Leclerc in [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Japanese_Grand_Prix), Verstappen suffered his second retirement of the season.[^cite_note-158]

In [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Mexican_Grand_Prix), he qualified in first place after setting the fastest lap-time of the session, before being handed a grid penalty for ignoring [yellow flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_flags#Yellow_flag) after a crash by Valtteri Bottas.[^cite_note-159] Verstappen suffered a puncture early in the race after making contact with Bottas, falling to the back of the field before eventually recovering to sixth place.[^cite_note-160] A third-place finish in the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_United_States_Grand_Prix) followed, before Verstappen took the second pole position of his career with a 1\:07.508 pole lap time in [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Brazilian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162] In a chaotic race, he passed Lewis Hamilton for the lead on two occasions before going on to claim his third victory of the season.[^cite_note-163] Verstappen ended the season with a second-place finish in [Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-164] Verstappen finished the 2019 season in third place in the championship with 278 points. He claimed three race victories, nine podium finishes, two pole positions, and three fastest laps.[^cite_note-165]



Verstappen at [pre-season testing in 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Formula_One_pre-season_testing)

Verstappen continued to race for Red Bull in [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Formula_One_World_Championship), alongside Albon. At the [2020 Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Austrian_Grand_Prix), he started second, but retired early in the race after a flywheel-related problem caused an electronic issue within the power unit.[^cite_note-166] Honda introduced countermeasures in response to the retirement.[^cite_note-167] At the [2020 Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), he crashed in wet conditions during the [formation lap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formation_lap) while he was on his way to the starting grid,[^cite_note-168] but he was able to drive the car back to the grid where his mechanics fixed the suspension of the car in the short time that was left before the start of the race.[^cite_note-169] After the repairs, Verstappen progressed from seventh place on the grid to second place by the end of the race.[^cite_note-170] Verstappen won the [70th Anniversary Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/70th_Anniversary_Grand_Prix) at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit), having started from fourth.[^cite_note-MVvolgensGR-171]

Verstappen clinched second place at the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Spanish_Grand_Prix), after qualifying in third.[^cite_note-172] At the [2020 Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Belgian_Grand_Prix), he scored a podium in third place, following his third place on the starting grid.[^cite_note-173] He suffered from two consecutive DNFs at the [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Italian_Grand_Prix) and [Tuscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Tuscan_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix after which he lost second place in the Championship.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] At the [Russian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Russian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen finished the race in second, his seventh podium finish of 2020.[^cite_note-176] At the [Eifel Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Eifel_Grand_Prix), Verstappen finished in second after qualifying in third.[^cite_note-177] He also managed to get the fastest lap of the race.[^cite_note-178] At the [Portuguese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Portuguese_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified third, however a poor first lap meant that he dropped back down the order to fifth place. He recovered to third and took his 40th podium in Formula 1.[^cite_note-179] At the [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix), Verstappen looked set to claim second due to [Valtteri Bottas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valtteri_Bottas)' ailing Mercedes slowing down, but a sudden puncture denied him any chance of a podium finish; Verstappen spun, resulting in his fourth retirement of the 2020 season.[^cite_note-180]

During Free Practice for the Portuguese Grand Prix, Verstappen was criticised for comments he made on the team radio after a collision with [Lance Stroll](42274711.md), where he used the words "retard" and "mongol" in response to the clash. Verstappen admitted following the session that the word choices he used were "not correct". The Mongolian government and the Mongol identity asked Verstappen to apologise for the comments; the Mongolian government also urged the [FIA](248019.md) to take action on the comments he made.[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] Verstappen finished the 2020 season in third place in the championship with 214 points. He claimed two race victories, eleven podium finishes, one pole position, and three fastest laps.[^cite_note-183]

#### 2021: Successful title battle with Hamilton



Verstappen at the 2021 Austrian Grand Prix

Verstappen won his first F1 championship in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_One_World_Championship). At the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Bahrain_Grand_Prix), Verstappen topped all the practice sessions and subsequently took a career fourth pole position. This was the first time he achieved back-to-back pole positions.[^cite_note-184] He fought Lewis Hamilton for the race victory, and on lap 53 Verstappen overtook Hamilton, but went off track whilst doing so, resulting in him being instructed by race control to let Hamilton back into the lead and ultimately finishing second behind Hamilton.[^cite_note-185] At the next race, the [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified third with teammate [Sergio Pérez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_P%C3%A9rez) second, marking the first time he was out-qualified by a teammate since the [2019 Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Italian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-186] At the race start, Verstappen was able to pass both Pérez and pole-sitter Hamilton to take the lead. He remained in the lead after the first round of pit stops as well as the restart, following the race being suspended on lap 33. Rival Hamilton finished second, reducing his championship lead over Verstappen to one point.[^cite_note-187]

In the following [Portuguese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Portuguese_Grand_Prix), Verstappen finished second after a long battle with Hamilton.[^cite_note-188] At the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Spanish_Grand_Prix), the battle between Verstappen and Hamilton continued, with Hamilton employing a faster two-stop strategy versus Verstappen's one-stop race. This provided Hamilton the advantage of faster tyres, allowing him to overtake Verstappen with several laps remaining in the race. Hamilton took the victory, with Verstappen taking second and the fastest lap, increasing Hamilton's championship lead to 14 points.[^cite_note-189]



Verstappen's 2021 title winning [RB16B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RB16B) in [Japanese](15573.md) colors white and red, as a tribute to their engine supplier [Honda](13729.md)

At the next race in [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Monaco_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified second behind [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc),[^cite_note-190] but Leclerc suffered a driveshaft failure on the way to the grid and was unable to start the race. Verstappen controlled the race from the front on the way to victory. Hamilton (who qualified seventh) finished seventh, though claiming an extra championship point by setting the fastest race lap. The result enabled Verstappen to become the [first Dutch driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_the_Netherlands) to lead the World Drivers' Championship,[^cite_note-191] by a margin of four points over Hamilton.[^cite_note-192] At the [Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified third behind Leclerc and Hamilton.[^cite_note-193] Verstappen and Hamilton passed Leclerc in the opening laps before Verstappen took the lead by way of a faster pitstop. Verstappen would comfortably hold the lead until lap 46 when he suffered a tyre failure causing him to crash at high speed and retire.[^cite_note-194] A mistake by Hamilton on the restart dropped him to last place, meaning Verstappen maintained his championship lead.[^cite_note-195]

Verstappen took pole for the [French Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_French_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-196] Verstappen's mistake on the first lap allowed Hamilton to take the lead which Verstappen retook during the pit stop phase. Verstappen pitted for a second time from the lead and set after the Mercedes duo, overtaking Hamilton for the lead on lap 52 of 53.[^cite_note-197] He also took the fastest lap point, extending his championship lead to twelve points[^cite_note-198] and achieving the first [hat-trick](13599687.md#Pole,_win,_and_fastest_lap_in_same_race) of his career.[^cite_note-199] Verstappen clinched pole position again at the [Styrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Styrian_Grand_Prix) and led the race from start to finish, to give him his fourth win of the season and further extend his lead to 18 points.[^cite_note-200]

Verstappen took pole position at the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Austrian_Grand_Prix), led every lap from start to finish, set the fastest lap, and won the race for his first career [grand slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_slam_\(Formula_One\)),[^cite_note-\:6-201][^cite_note-202] being the youngest to do so.[^cite_note-203] With the win, Verstappen also became the first driver to win three races in three consecutive weekends, starting at the French Grand Prix on 20 June, then the Styrian Grand Prix on 27 June and ending with the Austrian Grand Prix on 4 July.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] At the next race; the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_British_Grand_Prix), Verstappen was involved in a high-speed collision at the *[Copse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit#Circuit)* corner with Hamilton on the first lap. This resulted in a 51 [*g*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G-force) (500 [m/s<sup>2</sup>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre_per_second_squared); 1,600 [ft/s<sup>2</sup>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foot_per_second_squared)) impact with the barrier.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207] He was taken to the Silverstone circuit's medical centre after the crash and was then taken to Coventry hospital for precautionary checks and further assessment, before eventually being discharged at 22\:00 local time on Sunday night.[^cite_note-208] Hamilton would go on to win the race, reducing Verstappen's lead in the championship to eight points.[^cite_note-209] At the next race, the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen's car suffered damage in a multi-car collision on lap 1, where Mercedes driver Valtteri Bottas was deemed at fault. He ended the race in tenth which was promoted to ninth after Sebastian Vettel was disqualified. The outcome of the race allowed Hamilton to take the lead of the championship.[^cite_note-210]

Following the summer break, Verstappen qualified on pole at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Belgian_Grand_Prix), ahead of Williams driver [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)) in second and Lewis Hamilton in third.[^cite_note-211] The race was run for three laps, all behind the safety car, with the race official race results taken from the running order at the end of the first lap, with Hamilton and Verstappen both retaining their qualifying positions. As less than 75% of the race distance was completed, half points were awarded, resulting in Verstappen closing the gap to Hamilton to three points.[^cite_note-212] At the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Dutch_Grand_Prix) Verstappen again qualified on pole, beating Hamilton by 0.038 seconds.[^cite_note-213] During the race Verstappen was able to fend off attacks from both Mercedes drivers to take the win, taking the lead in the Drivers' Championship by three points.[^cite_note-RedBull.com-214] For the [Russian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Russian_Grand_Prix), Verstappen was required to start at the back of the grid for exceeding his quota of power unit components.[^cite_note-215] He made his way back up the field, and after taking an early pit stop for intermediate tyres late in the race, he finished second.[^cite_note-216] At the [Turkish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Turkish_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified second with Bottas on pole.[^cite_note-217] With the race being run in wet conditions and the drivers on intermediate tyres the whole race, Verstappen finished second behind Bottas, taking the lead in the Drivers' Championship by six points as Hamilton finished fifth.[^cite_note-218]



Verstappen battling with Lewis Hamilton for the lead at the 2021 United States Grand Prix

At the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_United_States_Grand_Prix), Verstappen took pole position in qualifying, edging Hamilton by 0.209 seconds.[^cite_note-219] Verstappen won the race and extended his lead in the Drivers' Championship to twelve points as Hamilton finished second with the fastest lap.[^cite_note-BBC_Sport_2021-220] At the [2021 Mexico City Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified third with a gap to pole-sitter Bottas of 0.350 seconds.[^cite_note-221] Verstappen's main rival Hamilton qualified second. At the start, Verstappen took the lead from Bottas and Hamilton into turn 1, and won the race; and as a result extended his lead in the championship to 19 points.[^cite_note-Fox_Sports_2021-222]

Verstappen and Hamilton each entered the final round of the championship—the [Abu Dhabi Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix) at [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit)—on exactly 369.5 points, with Verstappen leading on countback.[^cite_note-223] Verstappen qualified on pole position by nearly four-tenths of a second, but had a slow start and lost the lead to Hamilton into turn one. He attempted to re-pass Hamilton at turn six, who cut the track but was not penalised and maintained the lead. The stewards deemed that no advantage was gained, amidst Verstappen's and Red Bull's complaints. Verstappen was trailing Hamilton by over 10 seconds until a safety car was deployed with five laps remaining. The ending of the safety car and subsequent resumption of the race for one single lap caused significant controversy; race director [Michael Masi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Masi) allowed all lapped cars between Hamilton and Verstappen to unlap themselves, but no other. He also restarted the race for the next lap in supposed contravention to the rulebook. Verstappen passed Hamilton at turn five of the final lap of the final race to become the [first World Drivers' Champion from the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_drivers_from_the_Netherlands) and the [34th overall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Drivers'_Championship).[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225] Mercedes' protests were dismissed by the stewards[^cite_note-226] and Verstappen was provisionally confirmed as world champion, pending any appeal. Having initially lodged their intention, Mercedes later decided not to submit an appeal of the race results. Verstappen was named the best driver of the season by *[Autosport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autosport)* and *The Race*,[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228] whilst also topping polls of fellow drivers and team principals.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] [Mark Hughes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Hughes_\(journalist\)) of *[Motor Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_Sport_\(magazine\))* also ranked Verstappen first, describing his season as a "stunning combination of speed and racecraft, totally fearless, incredible tenacity and ability to comeback from adversity".[^cite_note-231]

#### 2022: Defending the championship



Verstappen at the [2022 Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Japanese_Grand_Prix), where he won his second World Drivers' Championship

In March 2022, Verstappen signed a five-year contract extension with Red Bull Racing for the 2023 to 2028 seasons.[^cite_note-\:3-232] From this season onward, he would use the number 1 instead of his regular number 33 as the reigning world champion.[^cite_note-233] Verstappen retired from second place at the [season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Formula_One_World_Championship)-opening [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Bahrain_Grand_Prix) with a fuel system issue. He won the [Saudi Arabian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix), having benefited from a pit stop during safety car conditions to pass teammate and pole-sitter Pérez, but suffered another fuel-related retirement from second place at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Australian_Grand_Prix). This left him 46 points behind championship leader Charles Leclerc after three races. Verstappen won the next three races; including sprint victory and a [grand slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_slam_\(Formula_One\)) at the [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-234] won from third on the grid at the [Miami Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Miami_Grand_Prix) while profiting from Leclerc's retirement at the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Spanish_Grand_Prix) to take the championship lead. Further wins came at the [Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix), where Leclerc again retired, and from pole position at the [Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Canadian_Grand_Prix). Verstappen qualified second at the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_British_Grand_Prix), but floor damage from running over debris harmed his pace and resulted in a seventh-place finish.[^cite_note-235]

Verstappen took pole position at the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Austrian_Grand_Prix) and won the sprint, but was overtaken by Leclerc in the race and finished second, reducing his championship lead over Leclerc to 38 points. Following this, Verstappen went on to win five races consecutively. Pole-sitter Leclerc crashed out of the [French Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_Grand_Prix), allowing Verstappen to win. Power unit issues meant he qualified tenth for the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Hungarian_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-236] but overtakes in the opening laps and pit stop [undercuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#U) meant he went on to take victory, despite briefly losing the lead after a spin.[^cite_note-237] He set the fastest qualifying time at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Belgian_Grand_Prix) but was demoted to 14th on the grid with a power unit components penalty. Despite this, he progressed to the race lead by lap 12 and went on to win.[^cite_note-belg2022-238] He took pole position and his 30th race victory at his home race, the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Dutch_Grand_Prix), and then won from seventh on the grid at the [Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_Grand_Prix).

Verstappen's winning streak was ended at the [Singapore Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Singapore_Grand_Prix). He ran out of fuel in qualifying and started the race eighth.[^cite_note-239] He lost places at the start and later had to make an extra pit stop due to a tyre lock-up, finishing seventh. Victory from pole at the shortened [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Japanese_Grand_Prix) gave him a 113-point lead in the standings with four races remaining, securing him his second World Drivers' Championship.[^cite_note-Morlidge_2022-240] He won the next two races, the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_United_States_Grand_Prix) and [Mexico City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix, finished sixth at the [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) after a collision with Lewis Hamilton, and won the season-ending [Abu Dhabi Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix) from pole. Abu Dhabi was his fifteenth victory of the year, breaking the record of thirteen race wins in a season shared by Michael Schumacher in [2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Formula_One_World_Championship) and Sebastian Vettel in [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Formula_One_World_Championship). He won the championship with 454 points, beating Lewis Hamilton's [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Formula_One_World_Championship) record of 413.[^cite_note-241]

#### 2023: Record-breaking third title



Verstappen leading the race at the [2023 Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Austrian_Grand_Prix)

Verstappen remained with Red Bull alongside Sergio Pérez for the [2023 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship). His campaign began with a win from pole at the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Bahrain_Grand_Prix). A driveshaft failure in qualifying at the [Saudi Arabian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix) resulted in a 15th-place start, but he gained positions in the race to finish second behind Pérez.[^cite_note-242] He again won from pole at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Grand_Prix) despite losing positions to the Mercedes drivers during the race,[^cite_note-Sky_Sports_-_wild_finish-243] but finished second to Pérez, who made a safety car pit stop, at the [Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-244]

After this began Verstappen's record-breaking win streak. He started ninth at the [Miami Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Miami_Grand_Prix) as [red flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_flags#Red_flag) in qualifying aborted his lap but passed the drivers ahead including pole-sitter Pérez to win. The next four races, the [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Monaco_Grand_Prix), [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Spanish_Grand_Prix), [Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Canadian_Grand_Prix) and [Austrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Austrian_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix, were won comfortably from pole position by Verstappen, including Red Bull's 100th victory in Canada,[^cite_note-245] as well as a sprint win in Austria. He took pole at the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_British_Grand_Prix) but briefly lost the lead to [Lando Norris](48978813.md) at the start before regaining the position to win. He qualified 0.003 seconds behind Lewis Hamilton at the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hungarian_Grand_Prix), but overtook him at the first corner and went on to take victory. He claimed pole and the win in the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Belgian_Grand_Prix) sprint and set the fastest qualifying time, but started the race sixth due to an engine component penalty. He went on to overtake Pérez to win the race. He won from pole at the rain-affected [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Dutch_Grand_Prix) and was victorious again at the [Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_Grand_Prix) despite qualifying behind Carlos Sainz Jr. This victory marked his tenth consecutive win, breaking Sebastian Vettel's record of nine from 2013,[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] and Red Bull's fifteenth consecutive win, a record previously held by [McLaren](20994.md) with eleven in [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Formula_One_World_Championship). Both Red Bulls were eliminated in Q2 at the [Singapore Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Singapore_Grand_Prix) and were unable to make an impression in the race, with Verstappen and Pérez finishing fifth and eighth respectively, ending the winning streak.[^cite_note-248] This would be the only break in Verstappen's success, as he went on to win the remaining seven races of the season. His victory from pole at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Japanese_Grand_Prix) gave Red Bull their sixth Constructors' Championship title.[^cite_note-249]

Verstappen clinched his third World Drivers' Championship title at the [Qatar Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Qatar_Grand_Prix) sprint despite finishing second to [Oscar Piastri](59449712.md),[^cite_note-250] then won the race from pole. He won the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_States_Grand_Prix) despite having started sixth due to a lap time deletion in qualifying. He qualified behind both Ferraris at the [Mexico City Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix) but passed both drivers to claim the win. He took pole and victory at the [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) and won the sprint having overtook pole-sitter [Lando Norris](48978813.md) at the first turn. He started behind Charles Leclerc at the [Las Vegas Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Las_Vegas_Grand_Prix) and received a penalty for forcing him off the track, but Verstappen reclaimed the lead to win. His pole and victory at the season-ending [Abu Dhabi Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix) was his nineteenth win of the year, breaking his own record of fifteen from 2022. He scored 575 points, more than double that of second-placed Pérez and beating his own points-scoring record from 2022.

#### 2024: Fourth title despite Red Bull struggles



Verstappen (pictured during the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Austrian_Grand_Prix)) won seven of the opening 10 rounds in 2024.

Verstappen began the [2024](72504121.md) season with his fifth career grand slam at the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Bahrain_Grand_Prix)[^cite_note-251] and followed this with another pole and victory at the [Saudi Arabian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix), his 100th Formula One podium.[^cite_note-252] He took pole again at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Grand_Prix) but retired on lap 4, his first retirement since the [2022 edition of the race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Australian_Grand_Prix), with a brake fire, ending his nine-race winning streak and 43 race-finishing streak.[^cite_note-race-report-253] This was followed by a pole and win at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Japanese_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-254] He won the sprint at the [Chinese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Chinese_Grand_Prix) after starting fourth,[^cite_note-255] then claimed pole and victory in the race.[^cite_note-256] He took another sprint win at the [Miami Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Miami_Grand_Prix)[^cite_note-257] and started on pole position, but lost the lead to Lando Norris during the pit stops and was unable to reclaim the place, finishing the race second.[^cite_note-258] He matched [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna)'s record of eight consecutive poles at the [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix)[^cite_note-259] and converted this into his fifth victory of the season. His pole streak ended at the [Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Monaco_Grand_Prix) where he qualified and finished sixth; Verstappen commented that his [RB20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_RB20) was losing time to its rivals in low-speed corners.[^cite_note-260]

Verstappen and George Russell set identical lap times in qualifying at the [Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Canadian_Grand_Prix), but Russell started on pole position as he had set the lap time first.[^cite_note-261] Both drivers were overtaken by Norris, but Verstappen later passed Russell then gained the lead from Norris during the pit stops to claim his 60th race win.[^cite_note-262] He qualified second behind Norris at the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Spanish_Grand_Prix) but passed him at the start and then overtook Russell to win for the seventh time in 2024.[^cite_note-263] Verstappen won the sprint from pole at the [Austrian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Austrian_Grand_Prix)[^cite_note-264] and then qualified fastest for the main race.[^cite_note-265] He led most of the race but was challenged by Norris in the closing laps before the two drivers collided; Verstappen was handed a time penalty for causing the collision and finished the race fifth.[^cite_note-266] He later commented that the crash did not affect his close friendship with Norris.[^cite_note-267] He qualified fourth at the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_British_Grand_Prix) and passed Norris in the closing laps to finish second behind Lewis Hamilton.[^cite_note-268] He started third at the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) but collided with Hamilton and went on to finish fifth. He criticised his team's race strategy and suggested that the RB20 was now behind the race-winning [McLaren MCL38](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_MCL38) on pace.[^cite_note-269] Verstappen qualified fastest at the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Belgian_Grand_Prix) but started outside the top ten due to an engine component penalty.[^cite_note-270] He recovered positions and was classified fourth in the race,[^cite_note-271] extending his lead in the championship to 78 points over Norris going into the summer break.[^cite_note-272]





Verstappen led 17 laps and finished second to rival Norris at the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Dutch_Grand_Prix).

At his home race, the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Dutch_Grand_Prix), Verstappen qualified second behind Norris and gained the lead at the first corner, but was later overtaken by Norris and finished second.[^cite_note-273] He qualified and finished outside the top four at both the [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Italian_Grand_Prix) and [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix,[^cite_note-274] after which Red Bull lost the lead of the Constructors' Championship to McLaren.[^cite_note-275] He then finished second to Norris at the [Singapore Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Singapore_Grand_Prix), reducing his championship advantage to 52 points.[^cite_note-276] Verstappen described his car as "fucked" at the official press conference in Singapore, for which he received a punishment from the FIA to "accomplish some work of public interest".[^cite_note-277] He responded by hosting an impromptu media session outside the press conference room in protest,[^cite_note-278] and later suggested that the decision could push him to leave the sport.[^cite_note-279] He won the sprint at the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_Grand_Prix) and was classified third in the race ahead of Norris, who was penalised for overtaking Verstappen off-track.[^cite_note-280] This extended Verstappen's championship lead for the first time since the Belgian Grand Prix. He started second at the [Mexico City Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix) but received 20 seconds worth of penalties for his driving during a battle for position with Norris, resulting in a sixth-place finish, reducing his championship lead to 47 points.[^cite_note-281]

> I mean honestly \[Russell and Norris] could have got a 10-second penalty and \[Verstappen] could have won by 30 seconds but it didn't matter. He was so calm about it; he was calculating. Every time he made a passing move. I mean he was exemplary. For me, this was a masterclass of how a racing driver reaches the pinnacle of his career and becomes a four-time World Champion.

— [Eddie Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddie_Jordan), speaking about Verstappen's victory at the [2024 São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix)

[^cite_note-282]

Verstappen was classified fourth in the [São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) sprint after receiving a [virtual safety car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_safety_car) infringement penalty,[^cite_note-283] and started the race from 17th following an engine penalty, whilst Norris qualified on pole. He gained seven positions on the opening lap, with further places obtained via several crucial overtakes, as well as surviving the worsening conditions on his intermediate tyres until the [red flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_flags#Red_flag) period.[^cite_note-284] Following this—and his overtake for the lead on Ocon—Verstappen set ten of the eleven fastest laps of the race, with his quickest being 1.045 seconds quicker than any of his rivals;[^cite_note-285] he won by a 19-second margin, increasing his championship lead to 62 points.[^cite_note-SP24stat-286] Verstappen was lauded by drivers and critics for his drive;[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289] Giles Richards of *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* opined that his performance was "worthy of recognition in the [pantheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantheon_\(religion\)) of Formula One's wet-weather drives", describing him as a *[regenmeister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regenmeister_\(disambiguation\))* (lit. 'rain master').[^cite_note-290] By finishing ahead of Norris at the subsequent [Las Vegas Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Las_Vegas_Grand_Prix), Verstappen secured his fourth world title with two races remaining.[^cite_note-291] Winning eight Grands Prix in all, Andrew Benson of *[BBC Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sport)* proclaimed it as a "towering achievement from a driver recognised as one of the all-time greats of Formula 1", describing his season as "almost flawless" whilst "not \[having] the best car for the majority".[^cite_note-292] Verstappen won the [Qatar Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Qatar_Grand_Prix), leading every lap after having his pole position stripped for driving unnecessarily slowly in qualifying; the incident led to a widely publicised feud with [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)). After the season-ending [Abu Dhabi Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix), Verstappen became the first driver to win a title driving for a third-placed team in the [World Constructors' Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Constructors'_Championship) since [Nelson Piquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Piquet) in [1983](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Formula_One_World_Championship).[^cite_note-293]

#### 2025: Runner-up to Norris

Verstappen driving the Red Bull RB21 at the 2025 Japanese Grand Prix

Verstappen received widespread acclaim for his surprise victory at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix) in [2025](72768531.md), ahead of both [McLaren](20994.md) drivers.

Red Bull trailed [McLaren](20994.md) for performance into [2025](72768531.md), with Verstappen finishing second at the season-opening [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Grand_Prix) after battling [Lando Norris](48978813.md) and [Oscar Piastri](59449712.md) for the lead in wet conditions.[^cite_note-294] He claimed third in the sprint and fourth in the main race in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Chinese_Grand_Prix) amidst tyre wear and performance struggles with the [RB21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Racing_RB21), whilst new teammate [Liam Lawson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liam_Lawson) finished outside the points;
